Review files deleted 2026-10-14 (kept tools/gemini_grammar_review/verdicts/: the rulings with reasons; its checker needs the deleted batch texts, the rulings live on in grammar_cases.json). **Residuals, round 2 (context clues, owner chose option 1):** fixed 8 of 12: ja-nai after an i-adjective / すぎ is a tag question (_ja_nai); typical-of らしい needs an adverb before (_TYPICAL_ADVERBS) or a noun after, else it credits hearsay rashii; ところを + 聞/探/知/教/尋 is a place; せられない (negative) is potential, not causative-passive; polite 限ります counts for ni-kagiru unless a person/group is limited (+ EXTRA_FORMS 〜にかぎる); past たものだ needs a time clue (よく/昔/頃 …); ことはない with a 彼/彼女は topic and no よ/ね/です is "never". Accepted (need meaning; hand-mark): できようが, 免れようと、 (you-ga-you-to), 灰にした (ni-suru), 到着するところだった (tokoro-datta). **Fresh accuracy test (2026-10-14, held-out; reviewed by me, not Gemini):** tools/accuracy/ (make_sample.py: all Tatoeba hits minus the review sample / grammar_cases / OpenJLPT examples, seed 20261014; record.py --report; sample.json + verdicts.json). Set A, 400 detections drawn as they occur in text: 394/400 = 98% correct (95% CI 97–99%). Set B, 50 per level: N3 39/50 = 78% (65–87%), N2 46/50 = 92% (81–97%), N1 43/50 = 86% (74–93%). Recall on real text not measured (only textbook examples: 74–83%). Systematic errors it found (not fixed yet, offered): ようとも/ようとは + しない = didn't even try (3), と+している = とする not として (必要としている, 常としていた, すらっと, 頑として: 4), ように + 注意 / という routed to ように言う (2), から見ると literal (2), ことごとく, 正直であれ。, たらどうかと言った, にせよと (quoted command), 森の中にあって. FIXED 2026-10-14 (25 of 28; residuals 得ようと願った, 恐いものがある, できもしようが); the sample (547 sentences) is now in grammar_cases.json (1,282 cases, 0 failures), so re-measure precision with a NEW seed. **Recall test (2026-10-14):** tools/accuracy/make_recall_sample.py (100 unseen Tatoeba sentences ≤ 40 chars, seed 20261015) + record_recall.py --report; every JLPT point used in each sentence annotated by me (recall_verdicts.json; points.txt marks the 45 never-searched points). Result: 374 grammar uses, 260 found = **recall 70% (95% CI 65–74%)**; 73% (69–78%) on searched points; 20 misses on never-searched points (passive/potential/って/中/さ/ぶり/とは/なしでは). Same sentences give a fresh precision check: 13 wrong of 285 detections (95%). Most missed (searched points): の possessive 14, explanatory んだ/のか/の？ 13 (only んです/のです are forms), で place 8, に indirect object 7 (only _GIVE verbs: 会う/聞く/告げる/命じる missing), は 7 (には/では/今日は), 方がいい in kanji 3 (only ほうがいい is a form), んですが softener 3, embedded か 3. New precision errors seen: お願いできますか → te-kuru (い+でき), いっつも電話してくる → te-iru/te-iku/ta, 一緒に行く → ni-destination, 途方にくれる → くれる, ようじゃないか → ja-nai + you-da, のではなく → ので. **Round 2 (2026-10-14): recall gaps fixed + re-measured on fresh samples.** Fixes: noun の noun (not only person の), は after に/で/と/から/まで/へ, plain explanatory んだ/のだ and verb + の？/のか, に + person for 会う/尋ねる/告げる/命じる/任せる…, で place with the verb later in the clause (not ですら/でさえ), んですが softener, 方がいい in kanji (EXTRA_FORMS), parser: 今日は mid-sentence = 今日 + は (was こんにちは), te-kuru not でき after a noun (お願いできる), ようじゃないか not じゃない, 途方にくれる, のではなく not ので, 一緒に not destination. Then precision fixes from sample 1 (として/とする, ようともしない → you-to-suru, ことごとく, すみません, から見ると literal, であれ。, たらどうか, にせよと, 森の中にあって, つくり出す, たらしい, 何時にしましょう, 与えよ, ますように → masu-you-ni). **Fresh samples (seeds 20261016 / 20261017; sample2.json, recall_sample2.json + verdicts):** precision A 293/300 = 98% (95–99%); B N3 45/50 = 90%, N2 46/50 = 92%, N1 46/50 = 92% (was 78/92/86). Recall 297/393 = **76% (71–80%)**, 78% on searched points (was 70/73); precision inside those sentences 307/314 = 98%. Still missed most: は/に after katakana names (トムは: the name is an unk token, so _is_word fails) 9+, の after 彼 / ほか / subject の 6, って (never searched) 5, sequential て without a comma 5, casual ている (住んでる, 言ってた, ております) 5, に + person when a noun sits before the verb 4, どこ 3, polite なければなりません/いけません, かもしれません, ても (浴びても). New precision errors: purpose ようと、 (3, credit you-to-suru when no question word?), 何といっても, 撃ち抜く, 埋め合わせ, 当たって/発って (たって), 次のように, 近いうちに, ご馳走になりに, ものじゃない credited as ものだ, 二度と (と), 来るという知らせ (named), 制限のあまりない. **Round 3 (2026-10-14): the six gaps fixed** (not re-measured yet; samples 1/2 show no lost correct detection). (1) names: the parser merges runs of unknown katakana into one unk token; grammar `_is_name` makes it count as a word/noun (トムは, ジムに). (2) casual ている from the parser's endings (てる/でる/てた/でた/てます/てて …) + EXTRA_FORMS 〜ておる. (3) sequential て before another verb (聞いて驚いた), not before ている/てくる-type auxiliaries (`_TE_AUX`), not in compound particles (particle + `_PARTICLE_VERBS`: について, に基づいて, を通して …) nor として / べくして. (4) `_tail` polite form of patterns ending in ない (いけない → いけませ, ならない → なりませ) + the detector pre-check anchor cut before the stem change. (5) purpose ようと、 with no question word / いくら / どんなに / たとえ credits you-to-suru. (6) って (casual topic after a noun, or a quote before 言う / 思う / 聞く / こと …), not after a cut verb stem (`_verb_te_split`: にわた+って). Also: ませんか not before ら; reason から not after a て-form (〜てから) and allowed after ません. Textbook recall N5 185→191, N4 220→223. **MeCab round (2026-10-14, done):** tools/accuracy/mecab_compare.py → mecab_diff.txt (CUT = we split inside one MeCab word; SWALLOW = one of our words contains a particle). Fixes: kana_ok.txt (tools/build_kana_ok.py: JMdict first-sense usually-kana + HAND incl. 物/言う/行く/所/訳/癖/一体; "any sense" was too loose: 浜 はま broke 外はまだ) lets those words match in kana and costs them like kanji words; kinds_fix.txt (tools/build_kinds_fix.py, MeCab, dictionary forms only + ない adj-i; it ignores the existing table when rebuilding) gives なる/くる/ない… a conjugation class; 彼の / 本の not read as かの / ほんの; 〜なさい conjugation rules; ように / ような are function tokens (was よ+うに "sea urchin"); この/どの before ような still credits kono-sono; both data files in the parse-cache key. MeCab disagreements: CUT sentences 97,082 → 73,807 after the kana fix (なっ 4,397→0, もの 3,950→0, いう 3,363→0, 彼の 5,413→0 …); the よう/なさい/ない round is being measured (mecab_diff_after_kana.txt is the previous run). Remaining CUTs are mostly place/person names and numbers (harmless). grammar_cases 0 failures, baseline updated, no correct detection lost in sample/sample2. **Round 3 measured (2026-10-14, seeds 20261018 / 20261019; sample3.json, recall_sample3.json + verdicts):** precision A 295/300 = 98% (96–99%); B N3 45/50 = 90%, N2 48/50 = 96%, N1 45/50 = 90% (sample2: 98 / 90 / 92 / 92, so no change within the CIs). Recall 315/411 = **77% (72–80%)**, 79% on searched points (sample2: 76 / 78); precision in those sentences 326/332 = 98%. The round-3 gaps (names, casual ている, って, sequential て) no longer top the miss list. Most missed now: に location (8: 冷蔵庫に, 郊外に, 家に泊まる, には), の (7: 誰の, subject の, 湯あがりの), た on some verbs (しまった, 欲しかった, 起きてきた), passive (5: される, はねられ, 分かたれ, 造られた), は (今日は at the start, のは), explanatory なの？/のよ, kenjougo (頂く, おる, 差し上げる), から from. Plain bugs seen: 彼はＤＪです misses です, 頂きます misses ます, しまった misses た. New precision errors: ひねくれた → くれる, ばかりに (only) → bakari-ni, わけには → ni-destination, 誰のですか → のです, 盗もう → もう, と比べる → to-and, 持ち合わせ / 埋め合わせ → au, ひとかどの → どの, 満月に → time, 自由にとって (取って), 身について, 抜かされ → nuku, うちに (home), なんか filler, 曲がりなりにも, のろまでもない, マジであれ/本気であれ/快活であれ。 → de-are. **Round 4 (2026-10-14, owner: "do the last round of fixes") — DONE, accuracy project can stop here.** Parser: 来た / きた / 来ない / きます were never read as 来る (the deinflector refused an empty stem except for する; now vs + vk); set phrases that are also verbs (しまった, いただきます) are the verb inside a sentence (`_phrase_only`, `_as_verb`); hiragana-only words from the frequency list alone cost 1.2 per kana for words of ≤ 3 kana (`_RARE_KANA`: たこ/とが/たが/かに/かも/くも/はむ swallowed particles: したことがある was し+たこ+とが, 妹はもう was はむ); Latin letters (ＤＪ) are one name-like unk token; もんだ / もんです never もむ+んだ; ばかり is a function token; -aru honorific verbs before ます (いらっしゃいます, くださいます, なさいます, ございます); kana_ok + 私 わたし, 早い/早く, 始める/始まる, 初めて. Grammar: passive is now searched (`_passive`: godan あ-row + れる / する-verb される on the verb's own stem; ichidan られる only with an agent に (a noun, not before は, not a na-adjective) / によって or + しまう; not いる/来る/なる/得る/spontaneous 思う 感じる …; short causative-passive 待たされる excluded only when the あ-row is before さ); kenjougo / sonkeigo searched from humble / honorific verb lists (`_HUMBLE`, `_HONORIFIC`; their patterns were only the labels 謙譲語 / 尊敬語; おる only as おります / 〜ておる, not とおり; not しなさい / てください; 参った！); adjective past かった → ta; が欲しい in kanji; godan volitional + とする (盗もうとしている) → you-to-suru; ni-location / ni-destination may take the clause's later verb (`_STAY`, `_PUT`; `_HAVE_ABSTRACT` 興味がある, `_NOT_PLACE`, ている after a て-form, にもほどがある, にかけては, ために, 以外に, 留守中に excluded); noun の + verb/adj + noun (僕の知らない人); を after a nominalizing の/こと; embedded か before 、+ 分かる/知る; ~20 false-detection guards (ひねくれた, ばかりに after a noun, わけには, 誰のです, 盗もう, 彼と年を比べる, 持ち合わせ/埋め合わせ, ひとかどの, 自由にとって, 身について, 抜かされ, うちに (home), なんか filler, 曲がりなりにも, のろまでもない, マジであれが, ときたら, からして、, がいっぱい, もう片方, 遊びに来る). MeCab comparison after: CUT 65,695 → 63,600 sentences, SWALLOW 27,291 → 26,155 (かの −421, かも −261, たが −210, とが −115, かに −95). **Fresh measurement (seeds 20261020 / 20261021, sample4 / recall_sample4):** precision A 291/300 = 97% (94–98%), B N3 47/50 = 94%, N2 48/50 = 96%, N1 48/50 = 96% (round 3: 98 / 90 / 96 / 90); **recall 343/405 = 85% (81–88%)**, 86% on searched points (round 3: 77%, 72–80%: the CIs don't overlap); precision inside those sentences 349/353 = 99%. Samples 3 and 4 + both recall samples are in grammar_cases.json (2,362 cases, 0 failures; baseline 3,888). New tools: tools/accuracy/probe.py (tokens + detections for sentences), recall_recheck.py (re-detect an annotated recall sample, --add-cases). Residuals (need meaning or vocabulary): 満月に (time?), literal ものがある / ものだ, 打ち合わせ / 駆け抜ける (lexical), なりにくい, 変えようとも考えた, というところだ, はたらいた / うたって (kana verbs read as たら / たって), 行かされた / 待たされた as passive, あれ、 (interjection), unknown kana verbs (うなずく, ひねくれる, のろま) split into pieces — the Word Addition Project. Most missed now (each 2–4 in 405): に location, explanatory んだ/の, て reason, も, こと nominalizer, causative せる, で place, が but. **MeCab dev tool installed 2026-10-14** (owner approved): fugashi 1.5.2 + unidic-lite 1.0.8 in %LOCALAPPDATA%\jlpt_devtools\pylib (251 MB, outside the add-on; use sys.path.insert). Not yet used for a comparison tool; idea: diff our token boundaries vs MeCab over Tatoeba to list parser misreads (今日は, 飛び込んで → 飛び+込+ん, いっつも, てこずる). MeCab/Sudachi idea (Gemini via owner): licences are fine (MeCab BSD option, IPADIC/UniDic-lite BSD-style, Sudachi + SudachiDict Apache 2.0) but it cannot ship in the add-on (compiled code Anki's Python cannot load, 50–500 MB dictionaries); it could be a dev-only tool (pip install, owner approval needed) to compare our word splits, useful for the parser-vocab to-do; it knows nothing about JLPT grammar points, so it cannot measure grammar accuracy itself. Possible later: a "not this sentence" exception in the grammar sentence search (offered, not built). grammar_cases.json 735 cases, 0 failures. **Next steps:** (a) restart the server (`.claude/launch.json` "grammar-review", port 47391 — keep that port, the ticks live in that origin's localStorage under `jlpt_grammar_review_corpus_v1`) and open http://localhost:47391/grammar_review_corpus.html; if the page is gone, regenerate it with the `--corpus` command in §5c (same sample, ticks still match); (b) owner ticks wrong hits level by level, I fix them, adding each ruling to `tools/grammar_cases.json` and running `grammar_eval.py` (0 failures, read the diff, `--update`); (c) quality bar: points still mostly wrong or ticked "never right" → `SENTENCE_SKIP`; (d) owner checks the deck search in Anki (untested there); (e) delete grammar_review_corpus.html at the end. - **⚠ TO FIX: parser vocab accuracy for the Text Check (owner 2026-10-14).** The Text Check tab runs the sentence parser over whole texts, so every vocab misread now changes a number the owner acts on (coverage %, verdict, the unknown-word list). Until now vocab misreads were mostly harmless: the owner's words come from word fields, and the parser only fed grammar. Seen on the first sample paragraph: 行き (noun, N3) read instead of 行く conjugated (行き、); 今日中 split 今 + 日中 (should be 今日中 or 今日 + 中); 企画書 → 企画 + unrecognised 書 (suffix 書 not handled); 切れ間 → 切れ + 間; ひとつ → unrecognised ひ + つ (kana spelling of 一つ blocked by the script rule). Each misread can show a word as unknown that the owner knows (or the reverse) and pushes coverage down. **Plan:** (1) a vocab test set like `tools/grammar_eval.py`: real sentences → expected words, run after every parser change (0 failures, baseline diff); (2) collect misreads from real texts the owner checks (a "wrong split" flag on Text Check rows would make this easy; not built); (3) fix in `sentence_parser` (costs, deinflection preference for verb stems before nouns, common suffixes 書/中/間/的, kana spellings of number words); (4) re-run grammar_eval too, since grammar uses the same split. Do this as part of, or right after, the Grammar Accuracy Project.

Today the heat score is the plain average of the learned cards' scores (`kanji_data.card_score`; grammar the same), so one strong card = many strong cards. Add a modest bonus for the number of learned cards/contexts (e.g. a saturating factor like 1 − e^(−n/k), or score × (0.8 + 0.2 · min(n, N)/N)), so a kanji/grammar point seen in several cards reads stronger than one seen in a single card; keep the change small and check the colour spread on the owner's deck stays sensible.

The Browser pane refuses `file:///` for files written by scripts, so it's served by `.claude/launch.json` "grammar-review" (python http.server on **47391**, tools/ folder); stop it after the review. Delete grammar_review.html once the flags are applied.

Overview (round 2 after the owner's screenshot: "looks really good, exaggerate more; light should break the box's bounds"): shared divine clock `_DIVINE` + `_divine_pulse(widget)` (widgets higher on the page breathe later → a wave rising from the badge), advanced by **`DivineOverlay`** (50 ms timer, child of the Overview's scroll content, mouse-transparent, idle below Amaterasu). Overlay draws over the whole page: breathing halo rings outside every gold `LevelBar`/`RankBar` (beyond the widget clip; bar fill/text untouched), a sunburst reaching past the badge, two counter-rotating ray fans (22 + 11 rays, per-ray width/length/shimmer, radial-gradient wedges, no pixmap masks), 4 drifting lens-flare ghosts.   - **Round 24 (2026-10-10):** owner asked why "A from your cards" (list words) ≠ "X of your N cards" (notes). Words vs notes aren't 1:1: variant/duplicate cards share a row (聞く+聴く), and a card can also cover a kana-written row. Relabelled "X of your N cards match a top-20,000 word" + tooltip. Some of those kana rows were wrong: `tools/build_freq.py` mapped kana corpus lemmas (mostly interjections/sound adverbs) to JMdict by seq tie-break (おっと → 夫, はは → 母, いえ → 家, すう → 吸う, さら → 皿). Now, for **hiragana** lemmas only, candidates prefer a JMdict entry whose POS fits the BCCWJ POS (`POS_FITS`), then one normally written in kana; with no POS fit the row keeps only its own kana (unless the entry has no kanji). Katakana excluded (it reshuffled loanword flags: バイ → 貝). `jmdict_parse.py` now stores `pos` (re-parsed `%TEMP%\data\jmdict.pkl`). ~76 rows changed; LIST_VERSION "pos-1". Owner's deck: words from cards 2,690 → 2,677. Kana いや row is now the interjection only (嫌 has its own row, 123 pmw). freq_extra.txt not rebuilt (LUW zip gone; display only).
  - **Round 25:** share card labels 19pt, clear of the glow; share bars got the LevelBar glow/sheen/laser rim (gold mode: deep-gold glow); freq bar label "Top 20k" + "near-native vocabulary ▲" goal marker. Chime is no longer a setting; seal checkbox only appears once Amaterasu is unlocked. Debug row: "☀ Force Amaterasu" (checkable, in memory: `_DEBUG_FULL`), which sets the previewed count to exactly 20,000 (kept on Refresh) and makes every `LevelBar` paint full (`paintEvent` swaps `_known`/`_pct` around `_paint`), Kanji & Grammar bars included. The debug row is hidden by default: an invisible 28×22 flat button at the row's left edge (under the rank description) toggles it (`_DEBUG_ROW`, in memory; `jlpt_gold_skip` keeps divine_text off it).
  - **Round 29 (2026-10-12): one tile per word (frequency list only; JLPT lists untouched).** Owner's rule: different meaning or use → own tile; same word with another reading or same-meaning kanji spelling → one tile. All hand decisions live in `freq_splits.py` (used by the build and by `freq_data.load()`):
    - Build (`tools/build_freq.py`, reads freq_splits from argv[1]): **ずる → じる** (32 verbs, 感ずる → 感じる, ずる kept as a spelling); **KIND1_SPLITS** (23 groups of BCCWJ lemmas JMdict files under one entry: 町/街, 足/脚, 開ける/明ける/空ける, 空く/明く→開く, 最も/尤も …) each its own row with only its own spellings (kanji forms sharing a kanji with it, kana of its own reading).
    - Load (`freq_data.load`): `_add_compounds` (freq_extra "e" ranks ≤ 6,000, ~340: でも, お金, やっぱり, 可能性 …, at the pmw of the row at that rank; spellings another row has stay there) → `_merge_reading_splits` (except **READING_SPLITS** 今日/汚す/避ける/汁, each keeping only its own kana) → `_split_shared_spellings` (**SHARED_SPELLINGS**, ~80 異字同訓 spellings UniDic counts as one: 聴く after 聞く, 帰る after 返る, 撮る/採る/捕る/執る after 取る …, parent's pmw, removed from every other row; `SHARED_WITH`) → `_fit_size` (trim/top up to 20,000).
    - Text coverage ignores shared and compound rows (`COMPOUNDS`, `SHARED_WITH`): their frequency is already in other rows (a first run without this dropped coverage 69.0% → 66.1%).
    - `meanings.lookup` uses `freq_splits.GLOSSES` first: 72 hand-written meanings for tiles whose JMdict entry is shared (速い/早い) or missing (聴く, 尤も …). These also show on JLPT tiles with the same word.
    - Hover: "rank #68 (frequency shared with 聞く)". LIST_VERSION "split-1". Owner's deck: top-20k known 5,123 → 5,176, coverage 69.2%. Rejected: Fix 1b (counting 帰る cards toward 返る).
    - Follow-up (same day, "split-2"): owner also split 有る/在る, 他/外 (ほか), 目/眼, 使う/遣う, 受ける/請ける (承ける stays with 受ける) as shared tiles, and 混じる/交じる as a KIND1 group (rebuild). Shared-spelling checks now go by (spelling, reading), so 外 ほか gets a tile beside 外 そと and そと keeps its 外. Still merged on purpose: 良い/善い/好い, 因る/依る/拠る/由る, 内/中, 付く/附く, 分かる/解る/判る, 言う/云う, 全て/総て.
    - **Sentence parser isolation:** (2026-10-13: the safe compounds are now added back, §5c) the parser's lexicon uses `freq_data.parser_rows()`: every non-compound row *before* the trim to 20,000. With the compounds in it, なので / ですが / 今や / それら swallowed grammar (ので, が, や …) and 日本語 (reading changed, no POS) lost が-subject; the trim also dropped rare words (オーロラ → オー). Checked on 3,000 deck sentences vs the pre-split list: 3 differ (2 are fixes: 身に着ける was parsed as 着く → wrong に-destination; 1 rare 敵陣). Sentence cards therefore still count 可能性 as 可能 + 性 (deliberate). `freq_splits.py` added to the parse-cache signature; GLOSSES part-of-speech text must use meanings.json's words ("する verb", "na-adjective", "Godan verb" …) since the parser reads them.
  - **Round 28 (2026-10-12):** usually-kana words show their kanji spelling: `_kanji_form(display, reading)` (JLPT alt_forms first, else the frequency row headed by exactly that kana; never by spelling alone, which made かく → 書く). Used by Shift+click Jisho (searches 掻く, not かく), the tile hover title (かく 〔掻く〕), the Missing table's reading column (blank for kana words before) and the frequency "most common missing" chips. 457 of the owner's missing JLPT words gain a spelling. Also: share-card bars fill under Force Amaterasu; mini widget rank picture on by default.

Unverified on his PC until he retests.

Rank search passes `seen_only=False` (it already needs is:review; Count all notes still counts new). History signature gets "seen-only:1". Legend "New / learning" → "Learning". Py3.9: `from __future__ import annotations` added to 9 modules (`X | None` hints crashed older Anki). Friend packages: `jlpt_progress.ankiaddon` (add-on folder) and Desktop `jlpt_progress.zip` (with `jlpt_progress/` folder), both excluding user_files, meta.json, tools, APP_VISION.md, root overrides*.json (the root overrides.json would be migrated into the friend's user_files).
  - **Round 22 (owner: お前 still missing in the frequency grid after Mark All):** a mark only reached a frequency row with the identical key, but the lists can spell a word differently (JLPT おまえ with alt お前/御前, frequency お前). `freq_data.mark_ranks(pairs)` maps marks through `word_rank` + the JLPT alternate spellings (the hover's mapping); `coverage` uses it for found and suspended. `found_words_in` identifies a word by its frequency row when it has one, so they're counted once. Owner's data after Mark All: 0 JLPT-linked frequency rows left missing. **Known, accepted (owner):** override files are global, not per deck — the debug "Mark All JLPT Found" on a test deck marks the real deck too (10,195 word / 2,383 kanji / 526 grammar marks as of 2026-10-10). The owner uses Reset All Found and will remove the debug row; no undo wanted.
  - **Round 21 — RULE CHANGE (owner 2026-10-10): words marked found count toward the rank, belts and Amaterasu** (reverses "the rank counts only learned cards"). `card_state.found_words_in(results)` = distinct found words with no card over the JLPT lists (`known_words` with nid None) and the frequency list (`found_ranks`), deduped by `_override_key`; kanji/grammar marks excluded. Every analysis stores it (`set_found_extra`: `__init__._open_progress_dialog`, dialog Refresh, mini widget) in `user_files/found_count.json`, and `rank_count` = learned notes + that count (also with `real=True`). History: rebuilt rank line starts at the found count (like the JLPT lines); snapshots carry `rank_v: 2` and older cards-only rank snapshots aren't reused. Owner's deck: 500 JLPT + 2,592 freq found → 2,651 distinct (= all found marks; 441 on both) → rank 3,170 → 5,821 (Nihonjin Blue → Samurai Black). Texts updated (Overview rank line + JA, "?" window, README). Grid marks reach the rank after a Refresh. Note: the "?" window's long English explanation isn't covered by the gold-text checker (built from a variable).

## 1. What this is

An Anki desktop add-on (`addons21/jlpt_progress`; working name **Kotodama** since 2026-10-15, opened from its own **Kotodama** menu on Anki's menu bar: Open Kotodama / Settings…; was Tools → JLPT Progress Tracker) that shows a Japanese learner how their Anki deck measures up in three complementary ways:

1. **JLPT coverage** — how many words on each N5–N1 list are in the deck.
2. **Kotodama 言霊 Japanese Rank** (was "Japanese Language Rank" until 2026-10-15) — a gamified rank (Gaijin → Amaterasu) from the number of *learned* notes in the deck (a young or mature card), toward 20,000.
3. **Frequency Check** — how many of the most common words in written Japanese the deck covers, so card count can't be gamed with obscure words.

**The owner** (Ben) is a Japanese learner and the add-on's author. He plans to share it for free with donation links. He works by sending screenshots and short instructions and expects concrete, verified changes. His deck: `Japanese::ベンの辞書`, ~3,403 notes (2026-10-08). His field mappings: note type **Kaishi 1.5k** (`Word` / `WordReading`) and **Lapis** (`Expression` / `ExpressionReading`). His live config is in `meta.json` (managed by Anki).

---

## 2. Philosophy (the "why")

### 2.1 There is no official modern JLPT vocabulary list — be honest about it
- The JLPT stopped publishing official lists in 2010. **Every** list out there (Jisho tags, japandict, OpenJLPT, stephenmk, etc.) is based on **Jonathan Waller's adaptation of the pre-2010 official lists to the N5–N1 levels**, published on his site tanos.co.uk. The official lists themselves were written by the JLPT organisers, for the old four-level test.
- So there is no "official + modern + complete" list. Its known problems: N3 was invented by splitting old levels, the vocabulary is frozen around the 1990s (ワープロ, テープレコーダー), it has no modern words (スマホ, メール), it tops out at about 8,000 words (too few for N1), and the scraped versions use archaic kanji (有難う, 此処).
- **Decision:** use the *best-cleaned* Waller-based list (**OpenJLPT**) for the JLPT bars, **plus** the Waller words OpenJLPT dropped and 211 words from community lists that Waller lacks (顔, 母, 頑張る …), all modern spellings (see §6 `ADDITIONS` / `COMMUNITY_ADDITIONS`). Handle the other problems with other features: the **Frequency Check** covers modern words and vocabulary size, **Suspend Dated Words** covers the 1990s words, and the **Rank** covers the size problem. Don't pretend any list is official.
- Other AI assistants have made exaggerated claims about OpenJLPT, for example that it "tracks post-2010 test data" (false) or that 男の兒 is a word (made up). Always check claims against the actual data.

### 2.2 Three lenses, each answering a different question
| Lens | Question | Counts |
|---|---|---|
| JLPT bars | "How much of each level's list do I cover?" | list entries matched to cards (+ marked found) |
| Rank | "How big is my vocabulary?" | **every note in the deck with a studied card** (learning, young or mature; `-is:new`), any note type, no field reading. Changed 2026-10-07 at the owner's request (it used to count every note); learning cards count since 2026-10-21 (like the JLPT bars and goal). |
| Frequency Check | "Are my cards the words that matter?" | deck words among the top 20,000 BCCWJ words (summary bar = Near Native 20,000; cumulative level bars Up to N5 … Up to N1 13,400) |

- **13,400 words ≈ the ideal vocabulary for comfortably passing N1.** The owner researched this figure, and it's where the **Amaterasu** rank begins (the rank threshold table says 13,402 because of rounding, but the UI says **13,400**). 20,000 = near-native, the max rank.
- The Frequency Check exists because "13,400 obscure words reaches Amaterasu without preparing you for N1."

### 2.3 Accuracy over inflation, and over strictness
- **One card = one word.** A card shouldn't inflate counts by matching unrelated homographs or homophones. Examples: 汚れ よごれ ≠ 汚れ けがれ, and a kana card そう ≠ 層/沿う/僧.
- **The same word counts wherever it's listed.** A card should satisfy every list entry that is *the same word*: alternate spellings (できる↔出来る), alternate readings (今日 きょう↔こんにち), お-prefix forms (お酒↔酒), する-verbs (あいさつする↔挨拶), and duplicate entries across levels (N4 舟 / N3 船).
- **Script rule (owner's explicit design):** a card written **with kanji** may count for a list entry written in **kana** (出来る card → できる). A **kana-only card never counts for a list entry written with kanji** (やさしい card ↛ a kanji entry). Knowing a word's kana doesn't prove you know its kanji spelling.
- **Words usually written in kana are listed in kana.** If JMdict marks a word "usually written in kana" (first sense), the list entry uses the kana spelling and the kanji becomes an alternate spelling (煩い → うるさい, 流石 → さすが, 眼鏡 → めがね; 275 words). Combined with the script rule, both kana and kanji cards count for these, and learners aren't pushed toward archaic kanji.
- When unsure, prefer verifiable data rules (JMdict, readings) over hand-written exceptions. Hand-picked lists (false friends, COUNT_AS_ENGLISH, NOT_ENGLISH, missing OpenJLPT words) are small and documented.

### 2.4 Learner-centric choices made by the owner
- **English loanwords** are understandable to English speakers, so carding them is bloat. → The **Mark English Loanwords as Found** button covers them, **excluding** wasei-eigo, false friends, abbreviations and loanwords from other languages.
- **Dated words** (90s tech, old spellings): the **Suspend Dated Words** button was **removed 2026-10-09** (owner: not worth it for 15 words). They're in `COUNT_AS_ENGLISH`, so the loanword button marks them found instead; their old suspensions were cleared.
- The owner's previous blunt "suspend all katakana" button was replaced by these two.
- **Licensing:** the owner explicitly said not to worry much about licenses (free app + donations) and to optimise for JLPT relevance. We still credit every source.
  - **BCCWJ (CC BY-NC-ND), decided 2026-10-12:** the owner knowingly ships the trimmed/re-ranked/JMdict-mapped `freq_bccwj.txt` and `freq_extra.txt` (derivatives; ND forbids that, and being free doesn't fix it). Accepted risk; worst realistic case is a takedown request. Plan B if asked: ship NINJAL's original SUW file unchanged (~8 MB zip) and do the trimming/mapping at load time in code; drop or replace `freq_extra.txt` (LUW source is 521 MB). Keep donation links voluntary and outside the add-on. A BCCWJ + Jiten blend (#12) would add a BY-SA vs ND conflict: ask NINJAL for permission before shipping one.

### 2.5 Gamification
- 10 ranks × 8 karate belts, an exponential curve (~1.05 per belt). Amaterasu's Black Belt is split into 10 degrees; the Tenth Degree is reached at exactly 20,000.
- **Amaterasu gold mode (20,000 cards):** every bar except the belt bar turns gold. That includes the JLPT bars, All Levels, the Frequency bars, their labels and the mini widget. Any new bar or label **must respect gold mode**, via `force_max_gold` / `set_force_max_gold` and `_set_level_label_gold`. A reward popup appears when crossing into 20,000; the edge is persisted via `rank_last_real_count` in the config.
- **Never show 100% unless truly complete.** 19,999/20,000 shows 99.9%. Use `jlpt_data.format_pct` / `format_progress` everywhere.

---

## 3. Feature inventory (current)

**Main window** (`ProgressDialog`, maximizable) has these tabs:

1. **Overview**
   - All N Levels Combined bar (white fill, red glow) and N5–N1 bars. Click a bar → a pop-up word grid (maximizable). Hover tooltip (2026-10-09): "K of T known / A from your cards + F marked found" (found = known entry with nid None).
   - Refresh button.
   - Japanese Language Rank: "?" info popup with dynamic list counts (Amaterasu art blurred behind a "?" until reached), debug row (±250, ★ Mark All JLPT Found, **Count all notes** (2026-10-09: checkable, config `debug_rank_count_all`, persists; `card_state.rank_count` drops ` is:review` so every note counts — Overview, mini widget, toasts, gold mode, Amaterasu popup — for previewing Amaterasu with a 20,000-new-card test deck; red warning under the rank text while on; `history.snapshot_values` passes `real=True`), Test belt-up / rank-up toast; the owner will remove these himself later), overall rank bar (fills left→right), belt bar (fills from the centre outwards), rank badge with art.
   - **Frequency Check:**
     - Description: the summary bar is the 20,000 most common words (≈ a near-native reader); the level bars show the **Required Vocab (Modern Estimate)** per level up to N1 ≈13,400 and are cumulative. (Wording of the cumulative explanation is an open to-do, §10.)
     - Summary bar: labelled **Near Native / 20,000** in the same 84px label column as the level bars (clickable → grid). Changed 2026-10-07/08 from the N1 13,400, at the owner's request; the 20,000 row was removed from the level bars below.
     - Stats line ("K known = A from your cards + F marked found · X of your N cards match a top-20,000 word · known words cover ≈Y% of everyday written text"). Changed 2026-10-09: the bar (cards + found marks, incl. loanword button) and the old "X of your N words" (cards only) looked contradictory (5,311 vs 2,692 of 3,364).
     - Always shown (the Show/Hide details toggle was removed 2026-10-07): one bar per **modern JLPT vocab estimate**: N5 800 / N4 1,500 / N3 3,900 / N2 6,000 / **N1 13,400** (no special highlight: the ◆ and outline were removed 2026-10-07) (labels **"Up to N5"** / 800 because the bars are **cumulative**, unlike the discrete JLPT bars; the description says so; bar tooltips add the discrete band, e.g. "words #1,501–3,900: 825 / 2,400"), all clickable → frequency grids.
     - The 100 most common missing words as chips showing readings for kanji words (#20 私（わたくし）); double-click → Jisho.
     - Source note.
2. **Word Grid**
   - JLPT list words only: **known words level by level (N5 → N1), each level grouped Mature → Young → New/learning (★ marked found last), most common first in each group**; then missing words from all levels. The subtext states this order.
   - Off-list deck cards are **not** here any more (2026-10-08); they live only in the **Off-list** tab.
   - Legend: level colours, "Missing JLPT", ★ marked found, ✕ suspended, and the maturity-saturation legend.
   - Search box (fixed 300px).
   - Tiles show the **first two characters at equal size** (10pt; one-character words stay 14pt).
   - Left-click: known → Browser (`nid:`), missing → **copy the word** (2026-10-09; marked found with no card → Jisho). **Shift+click: Jisho for any tile** (owner request 2026-10-08, since the hover shows only the first meanings). Ctrl+click: search the collection. Middle-click: mark found. Ctrl+middle: suspend.
   - **Hover = meaning** (#4, 2026-10-08): tile tooltips are HTML now (bold word + reading + **JLPT level in its colour** (2026-10-09, like kanji/grammar tiles; frequency-grid tiles look it up via `_JLPT_LEVEL_OF`, which includes alt spellings/readings, easiest level wins), grey italic part of speech, numbered first 7 meanings, "(+N more)", status, grey hints). Same meaning on Missing Words rows, Off-list rows (`_fill_table(..., tooltips=)`, `_meaning_tip`) and the frequency missing chips.
3. **Missing Words**
   - Columns: Rank | Level | Word | Reading. Rank may be estimated (≈#N, §6 `freq_extra.txt`); "—" = unranked.
   - Sort: "Most common first" (default) or "By level". Level filter, text filter.
   - Left-aligned; the reading is blank for words written without kanji (`_shown_reading`, also used by the Marked & Suspended and Off-list tables).
   - Copy all (copies the filtered, sorted rows).
4. **Marked & Suspended**
   - Found table with **★ Mark/Unmark English Loanwords as Found** under its header. **Show** (All levels / N5–N1 / Frequency list only) and **Sort** (Most common first (default) / By level / A–Z) dropdowns + Rank column + count line (2026-10-09; thousands of rows after the loanword button). Level lookup is one dict (`_level_map`), not a scan. Reset buttons tinted gold (found) / red (suspended).
   - Suspended table (header #e05a5a). (The Suspend Dated Words button was removed 2026-10-09.)
   - Reset buttons, per-row Revert, Refresh Progress.
5. **Off-list (N)** (renamed from Non-JLPT 2026-10-08): explanation (count, how many are in the top 20,000, "the lists are old and incomplete, many can still be on the test, they still count toward rank + Frequency Check"); **tile grid left | filter + Copy all + word list right in a `QSplitter`** the user can drag (grid starts at ~⅔ width).
   - Tiles grouped by maturity, most common first; the table is plain most-common-first. Ranks include ≈ estimates.
6. **History** (built 2026-10-08, backlog #3): QPainter line chart (`history_view.HistoryChart`, glow under a crisp line, hover crosshair + value box saying "Recorded snapshot" or "Rebuilt from your review history"), range buttons 7 / 30 / 90 days / All time (default All), group toggles Rank (learned + dashed mature, pink `#ff5c8a`) / JLPT levels (All = white with red glow, N5–N1 level colours) / Frequency (6 blues; off by default), "+N learned this week · +N this month", last-snapshot status, **Take snapshot now** (owner's request: full rescan + manual snapshot). The Overview shows "+N learned this week" under the belt bar (`results["history_week"]`). Chart lines ignore gold mode (they need distinct colours).

7. **Kanji & Grammar** (2026-10-09, #5 phase 1; `kanji_view.KanjiGrammarTab`): intro line (separate from words, no effect on rank), **compact** `LevelBar`s (`compact=True`: 36px high, 18px bar, 8pt text, half glow) with 70px labels: **All Kanji** (white/red like All Levels) + N5–N1; heat legend; Off-list kanji note; Grammar section (phase 2): All Grammar + N5–N1 compact bars (84px labels), "no grammar deck set" hint in manual mode; grids via the bars (`GrammarGridPanel`: learned strongest first → marked → rest in OpenJLPT order; unmatched grammar cards listed at the bottom). Grammar tiles: click learned → Browser `nid:…`, else copy the pattern without 〜; Shift+click → Google; middle/Ctrl+middle → found/suspended. Bars respect gold mode (real count at build; debug ±250 via `OverviewTab.gold_preview`). Click a bar → `open_kanji_grid` → `KanjiGridPanel` (subclass of `WordGridPanel` for its search box): per level, learned kanji (strongest → weakest, owner 2026-10-09) → marked found → unseen; All adds an "Off-list Kanji" section (not counted). `KanjiCell`: heatmap fill (`heat_color`: red #e53935 0 → orange 0.25 → yellow 0.45 → green 0.65 → teal 0.82 → cyan #22c8f0 1.0, blended in HSV; stops packed into 0.45–0.82 where most scores fall, after the owner found the first ramp too compressed; subtle tile gradient lighter(106)→darker(112); white text with a 1px dark shadow on every heat colour (owner), unseen = dark missing tile with level-tinted char, ★/✕ like words; white search outline. Hover: big char + level, meanings, on/kun, strength name + %, card count, best maturity, up to 10 of your cards with maturity. Click known → Browser `"deck:X" 字`; unseen → copy; Shift+click → Jisho `字 #kanji`; middle/Ctrl+middle → found/suspended in `user_files/kanji_overrides.json` (only for listed kanji without a learned card). Counts update on Refresh.

**Frequency grids** (click any frequency bar):
- Sections named by level band: N5 · #1–800 / N4 · #801–1,500 / N3 · #1,501–3,900 / N2 · #3,901–6,000 / N1 · #6,001–13,400 / Near Native · #13,401–20,000. Window titles "Most Common Words — Up to N2 (Top 6,000)".
- Within each section: known words grouped Mature → Young → New/learning (rank order inside each), then missing in rank order. Sky-blue palette (`STATE_COLORS["freq"]`); hover shows the meaning + rank. **Overrides enabled (2026-10-08, owner request)**: middle-click found, Ctrl+middle suspend; found = known (★, last in the known groups), suspended dropped from the band after Refresh; header shows "· N suspended".

**Search highlight:** each tile gets the **complement (opposite hue)** of its own colour, with saturation ≥ 0.85 and lightness ≥ 0.55, so it works on mixed grids.

**Other surfaces:**
- **Mini widget** on the Anki main screen: N5–N1 bars, a rank/belt line + learned-word count, and two thin full-width bars (overall rank, left-aligned; current belt, centred) with the big bars' colours and glow; a pulsing gold **rank-up banner** (with ✕) after a rank-up. Toggled in Settings (on by default) with **Include rank** (on by default) and **Rank picture** (`mini_widget_show_rank_art`, default off, owner request 2026-10-09; enabled only with Include rank): the rank section becomes a mini RankBadge — rank art (data: URI, cached per rank in `_ART_URIS`) at full width over the badge gradient/border, glow from `_rank_glow_params` scaled to ¼, white shadowed text, bars below. Previewed via stubbed `_widget_html`. JLPT results cached for 5 minutes (the rank is recounted on every render); click → the main window.
- **Rank-up toast** while reviewing (`rank_toast.py`), repeated as the widget banner on the deck list.
- **Tile saturation by card maturity** (`MATURITY_SATURATION` in `progress_dialog.py`: new + learning 0.25 (merged into one level and one label, "New / learning", at the owner's request), young 0.60, mature 1.0), with a legend in every grid; the tooltip names the state.
- **Settings** (`ConfigDialog`): deck, field mappings (note type + display field + optional reading field), mini widget toggle + **Include rank** (`mini_widget_show_rank`; both **default on**, owner's request), **Ignore cards suspended in Anki** (`ignore_suspended_cards`, default off), per-level custom list upload (one file per level overrides the bundled list). Saving keeps unknown config keys (`**self._cfg`).

## 4. Code map

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `__init__.py` | Entry point, menu action, config helpers. **Matching engine**: `collect_words`, `_find_match`, `analyse`, reading helpers (`_extract_reading`, `_reading_parts`, `_accepted_readings`). `analyse()` also computes `results["frequency"]`. |
| `jlpt_data.py` | `LEVELS`, `LEVEL_COLORS` (FG) / `LEVEL_BG` (single source of truth). List loading/parsing (5-column format), `ALT_FORMS`, `FLAGS`, `ALT_READINGS` (**merged** with `_merge` when the same word+reading is listed at two levels), `FALSE_FRIENDS`, `COUNT_AS_ENGLISH`, `NOT_ENGLISH`, `is_english_loanword`, `format_pct`, `format_progress`. |

Ordering helpers: `maturity_group`, `_jlpt_rank`, `GROUPING_TEXT`. `_mystery_pixmap` (blurred Amaterasu art). |

Called from `_open_progress_dialog`, dialog Refresh, mini widget fresh analyses, and a deck-list hook that makes sure one exists daily even with the widget off. `rebuild()` derives every day from the revlog (rank/mature) and note dates (JLPT/freq: first review, else nid date; marked-found = first day); `build_series()` merges recorded days (rank/mature if same ignore-suspended; JLPT/freq only if same `signature()` = lists + freq list + mappings + ignore-suspended). |

U+20000+; not 々), `kanji_in`, `field_text`, `card_score` (FSRS/SM-2, see §11 #5), `source_config`, `analyse(cfg, deck, nid_to_display, kanji_overrides)` → `{source, deck, stats{char: score/cards/best/words}, levels, offlist, known}`, `level_counts(kres, suspended)`. All 526 patterns match themselves. `analyse(cfg, grammar_overrides)` (config `grammar_source`: mode `sentence` (default, phase 3) / `fields` + deck + mappings; hand marks always count), `level_counts`. Overrides keyed by grammar id in `user_files/grammar_overrides.json`. |

The rank bar fills **left→right** (like `RankBar`); the belt bar is **centred and grows outwards** (`.centred`, like `BeltBar`). |

## 5. Matching algorithm (JLPT lists ↔ deck)

**`collect_words`** scans notes in the deck (and sub-decks) using the field mappings.
- The display comes from `_extract_words` (strips HTML, `[furigana]`, parentheses; takes the first word).
- The reading comes from `_extract_reading`, which handles plain kana, `漢字[かな]` and `<ruby>`. Separators between alternative readings (・、/ etc.) are **kept as ・** so "よん・し" stays two readings.
- Duplicate (display, reading) pairs are skipped. **28 of the owner's notes are exact duplicates** (聞く, よく, 高い…); they still count toward the rank.

**`_find_match(display, reading, …, kana_nids)`** returns `(nid, soft)`. **Script rule:** if the list entry contains kanji, kana-only cards (`kana_nids`, built in `analyse`) are filtered out of every step (`_allowed` inside `_pick` / `_same_word`). The duplicate-card pass skips kana spellings of kanji entries for the same reason. A card's reading field can list several readings, and any of them can match. `accepted` = the entry's reading + its JMdict alternate readings. Steps:
1. Exact display match (`_pick` prefers exact reading → phonetic/alternate reading → card without a reading).
1b. The list's **alternate spellings** (OpenJLPT other_forms + all JMdict kanji spellings): いちばん ↔ 一番, できる ↔ 出来る.
2. Kana-only card whose display equals the entry's reading.
3. Reading-field match, only if the reading is unique across all lists.
4. Honorific: the list has お/ご+kanji, the card has the bare form (お皿 → 皿). **Soft** (doesn't claim).
4b. Reverse honorific: the list has the bare form, the card has the お/ご form (互い ← お互い).
5. **Same word listed twice:** spelling/alternate spelling + one of the accepted readings, even if the card is already claimed. **Soft.** (N4 舟 + N3 船; 挨拶 at N4 あいさつする and N3 挨拶.)

**`analyse`** processes levels N5 → N1.
- Within a level it removes お+kanji entries whose bare form is also listed.
- Suspended words are skipped from counts but still claim their card.
- Marked-found words with no card become known with `nid=None`.
- **Claiming:** `_claim` marks the one reading an entry used; a card with several readings (何 なに・なん) can serve one entry per reading and is fully claimed once all are used.
- **Unlisted (shown as "Off-list")** = cards not claimed, not soft-matched, not partially used, **and not a duplicate of a JLPT word** (same spelling/alternate spelling + accepted reading, e.g. 掛かる when かかる is already counted).

**Different kanji = different word (decided 2026-10-14, owner; was ON before).** Steps 1b, 3 and 5, the Off-list duplicate check and the parser lexicon only use `jlpt_data.same_kanji_forms()`: alternate spellings that pass `same_kanji(entry, spelling)`, i.e. the spelling's kanji (glyph variants unified via `GLYPH_VARIANTS`, 々 expanded, a leading 御 dropped) are a subsequence of the entry's, or the entry's are a subsequence of the spelling's with each extra kanji standing for entry kana the spelling dropped (子ども ↔ 子供). So 会う ↛ 遭う, 作る ↛ 造る, 飛ぶ ↛ 跳ぶ, 長い ↛ 永い, 越える ↛ 超える, 体 ↛ 身体; old forms (會社) also stop counting (rare on cards). Reason: N2/N1 kanji questions test exactly that choice. `alt_forms()` is unchanged and still used for frequency ranks and the kanji shown for kana words. Frequency tiles are unaffected (BCCWJ lemmas lump 会う/遭う; the owner declined more splits).

## 5b. Frequency matching (`freq_data.coverage`)
- **Kanji cards:** the card's display equals one of the entry's spellings, **and** (if the card has a reading) a reading part is among the entry's readings.
- **Kana cards:** count as the entry spelled exactly in that kana (そう → そう). If no entry is spelled that way, they count as the **single most common** kanji entry with that reading (kana_fallback_used). This prevents homophone inflation.
- `nid_rank` (best 0-based rank per note, top 20,000) and `nid_rank_extra` (nid → (rank, estimated) beyond it) sort the Off-list views.
- `word_rank()` gives list words a rank via spelling + reading.

## 5c. The auto parser (sentence fields, #5 phase 3, 2026-10-09)

**Since 2026-10-14 the parser also feeds the Text Check tab** (whole pasted texts, not only the owner's sentence fields), so vocab accuracy now matters as much as grammar accuracy. Open misreads and the fix plan: §0 "⚠ TO FIX: parser vocab accuracy".

**Config.** Each `field_mappings` row may carry `sentence_field` (stored only when set, so old mappings and the history signature don't change). A row with `sentence_field` but no `field` is a **sentence card**: `collect_words` parses its vocab. `kanji_source.mode`: `vocab` (default; word cards give their word, sentence cards their whole sentence) / `sentence` (every kanji in all sentence fields) / `fields` (another deck). `grammar_source.mode`: `sentence` (default; sentence fields of the vocab deck's mappings) / `fields` (grammar deck); hand marks always count.

**Parsed-word ids.** One sentence note holds many words, but the analysis is keyed one word per id. Parsed words get `card_state.pseudo_nid(nid, i)` = nid × 1000 + i (≥ `PSEUDO_BASE` 10^14; real nids are ~1.7e12). `real_nid()` maps back: used by `_open_in_browser`, `history.note_day`; `with_pseudo()` copies the note's maturity onto its words (both maturity sites); history's "mature" counts real ids only. The same word in many sentences is kept once, from the most mature note.

**Vocab (`sentence_parser.py`).** Dictionary = freq_bccwj + JLPT lists with alternate spellings (32,751 surfaces, 0.16 s). Rules:
- Script rule: a kanji word's kana spellings are dropped, and kana only spells a word with that reading (なんか ≠ カボチャ).
- Conjugations: Yomitan-style reverse rules with types (`_RULES`: godan rows, ichidan, する/くる, i-adj, ます/ている/ちゃう chains, 行く special). A conjugated form must fit the word's POS from `meanings.json` (Ichidan/Godan/Kuru/i-adjective/する verb); words without POS only match unconjugated.
- Choice among entries for one surface: the word actually spelled that way beats an alternate spelling (家 → いえ not 内; 始めて → 始める not 初めて; 彼 → かれ not あれ), then unconjugated, then lowest JLPT level, then `PREFERRED_READING` (私 わたし, 一日 いちにち), then frequency. (Freq readings are UniDic's formal ones: 私 わたくし, 明日 あす.)
- Segmentation: whole-sentence lowest-cost split (`words_in`): kanji word 1.0, kana word 1.2 (2 kana: 1.6), `FUNCTION_TOKENS` (particles, です/だった/んだ/ます …) 1.1 and not counted unless also a list word, unknown char 2.5, punctuation free. Fixed the greedy version's fake words (ですか → すか, だったんだ → たん, はしていない → はし).
- A single kanji stuck to other kanji (建築家 → 家, 死期, the name 露伴) is consumed but not counted.
- **Compound tiles (2026-10-13):** the ~340 compounds freq_data adds as tiles are kept out of `parser_rows()` but added back in `lexicon()` when `_compound_ok`: kanji compounds (可能性, 日本語), katakana words, お〜 nouns (お客様), 〜方 (考え方), plurals (私たち), さ-nouns (高さ), compound verbs with POS (位置付ける). Left split: hiragana-only ones (なので, ですが, それら: they swallowed grammar), ones ending in a particle (今や, 二人とも), and ones the parser already reads as a conjugated verb (行ける, 思える: 学校に行ける needs 行く for ni-destination). Only spellings no list word has; priority below list words. In `tokens()` a compound (`_COMPOUND_KEYS`) may not touch another kanji on either side (住人たち ≠ 住 + 人たち, 大学生活 = 大学 + 生活). Their POS comes from meanings.json (a compound without POS lost が-subject: 日本語が). Effect on the owner's 3,142 sentences: 120 compounds read whole; grammar fixes only (三人の子 no longer possessive の, 見た目 no longer past た; 異常な暑さ / 圧倒的な now na-adjective).
- Owner's 3,373 sentences parse in ~1.3–2 s (cached per text).

**Grammar (`grammar_data.detectors` / `sentence_ids`).** Each written form → regex: literal parts joined by a gap (`_GAP`, ≤25 chars, never crossing spaces/punctuation; お/ご patterns ≤6), last part conjugates (`_tail`: する/くる/行く/ます/です/だ (not で)/godan rows/る/い; a final で can't be followed by す/し), leading て/た also voiced after a verb (except てある: である). Only distinctive forms: core ≥3 chars or 2 with kanji, and not a shared key. Forms starting with 〜 need a real character before them; pure "Verb-stem + …" formations (`_stem_only`: 出す, 合う, 切る, 直す, かける, ながら, ます …) need an i/e-row hiragana stem end (`_STEM_END`, no に; kanji-only stems like 見 are missed on purpose); kanji-initial ones can't follow a kanji (実際, 週末). Containment: a match inside a longer point's match is dropped. Hand-tuned on the owner's sentences: `_NOT_AFTER` (として after う, ばかりだ after た/だ, ところに/へ/を/だ after の, わりに after 終/代/変/替, がたい after り, ものだ/もので after の, あまり after particles, 以上 after れ, ものの after う, と言う after 度, のです after も), `_NOT_BEFORE` (てある + がと, ことなく + て, もう〜た + い/く/か/け), `_SKIP_FORMS` {かった}, `_SKIP_PATTERNS` (〜なくして(は), 〜なしに(は), 〜ともなく, 〜ものを, 〜をもって, 〜上（に）, 〜手前, 〜にして, 〜を〜として, 〜上で: every hit was something else). Result on the owner's sentences: 503 detectors for 414 points, 138 points found, 0.2 s. Known leftovers judged acceptable: 〜を通して (糸を通す), 〜までに (ここまでにしましょう), あそこ in じゃあそこ, 〜に限る and 〜に限り both on に限ります.

**Context checks on regex detectors (`grammar_data._CONTEXT`, 2026-10-09).** id → fn(text, match) returning the id to credit (possibly another point) or None. First one: `you-ga-you-to` (N1 concessive 〜(よ)うが/うと) only with 、/も/end after it or a question word (`_QUESTION_WORDS`, incl. たとえ) earlier in the clause; otherwise ようと + next action (握手をしようと手を差し出した) credits `you-to-suru`, and a bare ようが credits nothing. Owner's deck: N1 hit 1 → 0 (it was that false one), you-to-suru 5 → 6. Known miss (not a false credit): godan volitional + うと/うが (降ろうと, あろうとも) isn't searched, since 2-kana cores are skipped.
**Owner's review page round 1 (2026-10-14):** context points now try every match (`finditer`), not just the first. Leading voiced で/だ only after ん/い (緩んできた, 泳いだ): できました / 何で来ない were te-kuru; side fixes: これでいく, テレビで見た. `_aida_ni`: not 間に合う, いつの間に / 瞬く間に / あっという間に / 束の間, nor AとBの間. `_mitai`: not a verb's own み (住みたい: み inside the parser's token) nor 〜てみたい. Owner ticked 緩んできた / 飛び込んできた / その間に too, but those are real uses (kept; told the owner). Owner's deck: 30 wrong credits removed.
**Round 2 (N4, 2026-10-14):** `_tail` for する: bare し/さ/せ only before a real する ending (`し(?=[まてたなよろちつ])`, `さ(?=[せれな])`, `せ(?=[ずよん])`; しっぽ, おがさわら, くさい, くせに, たくさん were する). お〜 gap is `_O_GAP` (one stem or a 2–3-kanji noun, no particles; was any 1–6 chars: お腹が空きまし). `_NOT_AFTER` までに: ここ/そこ/どこ. New `_CONTEXT` (helper `_token_at`): `_dasu` (a verb stem before 出す, not 出す alone; `_DASU_LEXICAL` 思い/取り/呼び/差し/引き/生み/抜け … rejected), `_koto_ga_aru` (dictionary form or ない before こと), `_o_suru` (not お願いする), `_ku_suru` (the parser's adjective く, or a kanji く-word whose い-form is an i-adjective: 早くしろ; known miss: 仲良く), `_ga_suru` (`_SENSES` before が: 音, 声, 匂い, 味, 予感 …; 気がする credits N3 ki-ga-suru), `_naide` (not after では/じゃ; not sentence-final or before くれ/ください/ね/よ). ご〜する isn't searched (pattern is お〜する only). Owner's deck: 186 points (koto-ga-aru and made-ni had only false hits).
**Round 3 (N3–N1, 2026-10-14):** 20 more `_CONTEXT` checks (helpers `_before/_after/_ends_clause`, `_SENT_END`): mono-dakara (not もので+は/も, not after もの(では)なく), mono-de-wa-nai (not ものではなく), koto-da (dict/ない before, clause end after, no topic は in the sentence), koto-wa-nai (not なかった), ni-tsuite (not ついていく/ついてる, 位置について), you-na (not ようなんだ/なのだ), to-shite (not ひょっとして), tara-ta (sentence's last predicate past; spaces don't end the sentence), tatte-datte (not んだって/のだって, not clause-final, not before 聞/言/思/顔/有名 … quotes), ba-ii (only ばいい, not ければいけ), muki-muke (now allowed after kanji: `_NOUN_SUFFIX`; not after a particle, `_MUKI_LEXICAL` 前向き/風向き, nor verb 向けた/て), kiru (`_KIRU_LEXICAL` 締め切り/張り切る/思い切り …, not 切り+kanji), kakeru (`_KAKERU_LEXICAL` 話しかける/呼びかける/追いかける …), au (`_AU_LEXICAL` 知り合い/付き合う/待ち合わせ/組み合わせ …), を通して (not 糸/針 … threading), ni-kagiru (plain に限る only, not after time words), ni-kagiri (not 限ります), ni-sureba (person/group before: `_person`, `_GROUP_END`), ni-mukete (`_PHYSICAL_AIM` 的/方向/東 …), nagara-ni (`_NAGARA_NOUNS` 涙/居/昔/生まれ/いつも/今更 only). Owner's deck: 174 points; 14 points now have no detections (all their hits were flagged). 獲得するものだから now credits mono-da (general truth), arguably right. Known misses: 読みかけの (かけの isn't searched), 仲良くする.
**Accuracy programme (owner 2026-10-14: "how many false positives for points I don't have sentences for, let alone false negatives"; chose all three options):**
  1. **`tools/grammar_eval.py`** (no Anki needed; run after every grammar/parser change): recall on OpenJLPT's examples (`tools/grammar_examples.json`, 1,526 sentences, all examples not just the first), the owner's rulings (`tools/grammar_cases.json`, 187 sentences: "absent" = owner flagged, "present" = real uses; must show 0 failures), and a per-sentence diff against `tools/grammar_eval_baseline.json` (`--update` to accept). First run: points detecting any own example N5 91% / N4 89% / N3 82% / N2 92% / N1 87%; own examples detected 74–83%. 45 points are never searched. Examples are textbook-clean, so real recall is lower.
  2. **Tatoeba review:** `tools/grammar_review.py --corpus %TEMP%\data\tatoeba\jpn_sentences.tsv` (Tatoeba jpn export 2026-10-10, 3.4 MB bz2, 248,924 sentences, CC BY 2.0 FR; owner approved the download) → `tools/grammar_review_corpus.html` (own localStorage key; fixed md5 sample, up to 8 per point; "N hits in Tatoeba"). 453 points detected; ~2.8 min. Owner reviews it level by level like the deck page.
  3. **Quality bar (to do after the review):** points whose hits stay mostly wrong after fixing, or ticked "never right", go into `SENTENCE_SKIP` (hand-mark only).
  - **Find it in your deck** (owner's idea, to make hand marking easy): `grammar_data.search_regex(gid)` = every form of the pattern as one Rust-compatible regex (〜 dropped, variants OR-ed, `.{0,25}?` between parts, voiced て/た, conjugating last part: する/くる/ます/です/だ/ない (+ polite ません/ありません, a→i row)/godan-ichidan-i endings left open). `kanji_view.search_grammar_in_deck` runs `"deck:<config deck_name>" "re:<regex>"` (tooltip if nothing matches). Grammar tile: click on a not-learned point, or Ctrl+Click on a learned one; Missing → Grammar: click a row (was: copy the pattern). The textbook example matches for all but 9 points (尊敬語/謙譲語 categories, potential, volitional, ことは〜が, 至る, 堪える, をもって). **Unverified in Anki** (Anki's regex search can't run headlessly).

**Short grammar from tokens (`grammar_data.token_ids`, 2026-10-09).** `sentence_parser.tokens(text)` returns the whole split: (surface, kind word/func/lone/punct/unk, display, reading, conjugation type, endings undone) — `_deinflect` now records the endings path; 〜たら rules added; particle pairs (には, では …) removed from `FUNCTION_TOKENS` so they split. Single-meaning points credit when the token stands alone (は after a word, ね/よ at clause end, です, だ before an end/よ/ね, この + word …) or from the endings path (ます, ない, た, たい, たら, ば, volitional, imperative, くて, adj そう). Shared forms only with clear context (`_TIME_END`, `_PLACE_END`, `_PERSON`, `_MOVE`, `_EXIST`, `_GIVE`, `_MEANS`, `_WITH`, `_KNOW`, number+counter): に time / destination / place / indirect object / frequency, で means / place, が subject / but, か question / embedded / or, と and / with / conditional, から from / reason, まで until, も also / emphasis, の possessive / nominalizer, な na-adj / prohibition (not after an i-adjective: 心細いな), のに, ながら, しか〜ない vs しかない, にする decide (not 気にする) / make, そうだ, らしい, ように, くらい. Every rule was read against samples from the owner's sentences. Result: grammar 133 → **189** learned (N5 70/81, N4 66/98); analysis 2.4 s with grammar.

**Owner decisions (2026-10-09):** no "label your sentences" review (word-card users don't really study the sentence; marking found is faster and more honest). Missing → Grammar has a "why are obvious points here" note, **★ Found** per row (kanji too; `KGMissingPanel.changed` → `_on_override_changed`) and **★ Mark basic particles as found** toggle (`BASIC_PARTICLES`: the owner's list は, が subject, を, に destination/time/indirect object, で place/means, の, も, と and/with, へ, か question, ね, よ; と quotation has no separate point). The grammar-section note and the source line under the bars were removed at the owner's request (2026-10-09); Each section header now just states its source (`_source_text` / `_grammar_source_text`, from `field_labels`): "parser · word fields: Kaishi 1.5k → Word, Lapis → Expression", "parser · sentence fields: … / none set (add one in Settings → Deck & Fields)", or "field: <note type> → <field> (deck …)".

## 6. Data pipeline (how the bundled files are made)

All bundled data files (`jlpt_n*.txt`, `freq_bccwj.txt`, `freq_extra.txt`) are **generated**; don't hand-edit them. Fix the build script and rebuild. Scripts are in `tools/`, and they expect to be run **from a working folder containing `data/` and `ojlpt/`**: currently `C:\Users\benne\AppData\Local\Temp\` (`data/jmdict.pkl`, `data/JMdict_e.gz`, `data/bccwj_suw.zip`, `ojlpt/n1..5.json`). `%TEMP%` can be cleaned, so re-download if missing. Run with `python -I -X utf8 tools/<script>.py <addon_dir> …`. Before changing a build, confirm it reproduces the current files byte-for-byte (it did on 2026-10-08).

**Sources**
| Data | Source | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| JLPT lists | OpenJLPT `data/json/vocab/n{1..5}.json` (https://github.com/evanclan/OpenJLPT, CC BY-SA 4.0) | Waller levels; fields word, reading, other_forms, jmdict_id, meanings, examples |
| Dictionary | JMdict_e.gz (http://ftp.edrdg.org/pub/Nihongo/JMdict_e.gz, ~10.6 MB, CC BY-SA 4.0) | `tools/jmdict_parse.py` → `data/jmdict.pkl` (keb, reb, lsource langs, wasei flag, misc) |
| Frequency | BCCWJ short-unit list v1.0 (NINJAL record 3234, `BCCWJ_frequencylist_suw_ver1_0.zip`, ~8 MB, CC BY-NC-ND 3.0) | Direct link 403s without a browser referer; use `curl -e https://repository.ninjal.ac.jp/records/3234 -A Mozilla/5.0 https://repository.ninjal.ac.jp/record/3234/files/<file>` |
| Meanings | Jitendex for Yomitan (jitendex.org downloads page → `https://github.com/stephenmk/stephenmk.github.io/releases/latest/download/jitendex-yomitan.zip`, 37 MB, CC BY-SA 4.0; version 2026-10-03 used). Kept at `%TEMP%\data\jitendex_dl\`. | `tools/build_meanings.py <addon_dir> <zip>` → `meanings.json`. Words from `jlpt_n*.txt` + `freq_bccwj.txt` only (not freq_extra). Match term = display or alt spelling AND reading (kata=hira); する/と/に suffix fallback (コピーする → コピー; あいさつする → alt 挨拶 + あいさつ); highest Yomitan score among positive seqs; negative seqs are redirects (ウエートレス ⟶ ウェイトレス), followed. Senses from `sense-group` (part-of-speech titles, shortened via `_POS_SHORT`: する verb, Godan verb, na-adjective …) and `sense` → `glossary` li's, else `info-gloss-content`. 2026-10-08: 21,315 words → 20,900 entries, 17 unmatched (set phrases, brand names). Owner's deck: every known JLPT word has a meaning; 335/771 Off-list cards. |
| Compound ranks | BCCWJ long-unit list v1.0 (NINJAL **record 3228**, `BCCWJ_frequencylist_luw_ver1_0.zip`, 57 MB zip / 521 MB TSV) | Same download trick with record 3228. Last copy was in a session scratchpad (gone after reset). Neighbouring records: 3229 luw v1.1, 3230/3231 luw2, 3235 suw v1.1. |
| Waller reference | stephenmk/yomitan-jlpt-vocab `original_data/n{1..5}.csv` (jmdict_seq, kana, kanji, waller_definition) | Waller's own data + JMdict ids. tanos.co.uk doesn't resolve from the owner's network. |
| Community lists | jamsinclair/open-anki-jlpt-decks `src/n*.csv`, elzup/jlpt-word-list `src/n*.csv` | Jisho tags + older Waller versions + textbook tags (Genki); source of `COMMUNITY_ADDITIONS`. |

**`tools/build_jlpt.py <addon_dir>`** → `jlpt_n*.txt`
- Format: `word<TAB>reading<TAB>alt spellings (|)<TAB>flags (,)<TAB>alt readings (|)`.
- Alternate spellings = OpenJLPT other_forms + **all JMdict kanji spellings** (4,338 added).
- Alternate readings = all JMdict readings of the entry (1,607 added).
- Flags (katakana words only): `eng`, `wasei`, `abbr`, `loan-<lang>`. JMdict usually omits lsource for English loanwords, so no lsource is treated as eng.
- **Usually-kana words are relisted in kana:** if JMdict's first sense has misc "word usually written using kana alone" and the word has kanji, then the display becomes the reading and the kanji moves to the front of the alternate spellings. That's 275 words. Several kanji variants can collapse into one kana entry (叔父さん/伯父さん/小父さん → おじさん), which the loader dedupes.
- **`ADDITIONS`** dict: corrections for words OpenJLPT is missing. N5 私 わたし (JMdict 1311110), plus (2026-10-08) **39 Waller words OpenJLPT dropped**, found by diffing against stephenmk/yomitan-jlpt-vocab `original_data/n*.csv` (Waller's levels + JMdict ids; tanos.co.uk itself doesn't resolve from the owner's network). Owner's rules: modern spellings only, no archaic words, single-kanji readings only when JMdict has their own entry (上 かみ, 下 しも/げ, 万 ばん, 無 ぶ added; 依 い, 仮 か … skipped). Not added: stephenmk id mistakes (金棒, 金蔵, 伝言 つてこと), rare/odd (僅々, 煌々, 秭 …). (私 わたし: OpenJLPT only had 私 わたくし (N5) and あたし (N1); JMdict keeps わたし and わたくし as separate entries.) The diff scripts lived in a session scratchpad: compare by JMdict id + (spelling, reading incl. alt forms/readings) against the bundled lists, map to JMdict with ke_inf/ke_pri to pick modern spellings and flag archaic words, strip bracket hints "(みずを～)" and skip affixes "～派".
- Current counts (2026-10-14, after the first `ours` additions): N5 683, N4 640, N3 1,704, N2 1,830, N1 3,213 (8,070). `OUR_ADDITIONS` in tools/build_jlpt.py (flag `ours`) holds words on no list with levels we chose; build_jlpt needs `%TEMP%/data/jmdict.pkl` + `%TEMP%/ojlpt/` (run from %TEMP%, output folder as argument; build to a scratch folder and diff first).
- **`COMMUNITY_ADDITIONS`** (2026-10-08): **all 211** candidates from jamsinclair/elzup (13 modern-N-tagged, 188 old-test-tagged, 10 set phrases), owner's call: "set phrases like 仕方がない aren't understandable from their parts". Easiest level when the lists disagree. Each gets the list-file flag **`community`** (column 4; harmless to loanword logic, `eng` still added for katakana). Build entries may now carry their own `flags`.
- **Waller's own lists lack** 顔, 母, 頑張る, 当然, 迷う, 勝手, ない, 日本語 (英語 *is* N5); OpenJLPT and stephenmk agree. Likely reasons (unverified): ない is grammar (negation) / the negative of ある; 日本語 is 日本 (a name) + 〜語. The community lists filled most of these; **日本語 is still on no list.**
- **Other community lists checked (2026-10-08):** jamsinclair/open-anki-jlpt-decks and elzup/jlpt-word-list (near-identical; built from Jisho tags + older list versions + textbook tags such as Genki). After removing formatting variants ("足; 脚", "運動(うんどうする)") and 275 affixes (～派, 故～): **13 confident** (both lists, modern N-level tag): N5 ない, 顔, 母, 父, もしもし; N4 専門, 頑張る, スーパー, パパ, 正月, 失礼, お子さん, ハンバーグ. **188** carry only old-test tags (JLPT_1/JLPT_3 …), so their N-level is the list makers' guess (e.g. 当然/迷う N3, 自宅/叔父 N2, 勝手/実家/魂 N1). **10** are set phrases (行ってきます, どういたしまして …). **All 211 added** (see `COMMUNITY_ADDITIONS`).

**`tools/build_freq.py <addon_dir>`** → `freq_bccwj.txt`
- Format: `display<TAB>reading<TAB>per-million<TAB>spellings (|)<TAB>loanword flag`; line order = rank. Top 20,000. The 5th column (added 2026-10-08; columns 1–4 verified unchanged) uses build_jlpt's rules on the chosen JMdict entry for katakana displays: 2,139 eng, 316 abbr, 8 wasei, 229 loan-xxx. JMdict tags few wasei-eigo, so `FALSE_FRIENDS` matters even more here.
- Keeps content POS only: nouns-普通名詞, verbs, adjectives, 形状詞-一般/タリ, adverbs, 連体詞, 接続詞, 感動詞-一般, pronouns. Drops particles, auxiliaries, affixes, numerals and proper nouns. Lemma must be Japanese characters only.
- **The laws register (OL) is excluded.** It's ~1% of the corpus but heavily repeats legal jargon (当該 was #588 → now #1,475; 条, 項, 規定). Only 12 law-heavy words are on JLPT lists, and they're all still within the top 13,400.
- Each (lemma, reading) maps to **one** JMdict entry: prefer lemma as kanji spelling, then the earliest position of the reading. The display is the entry's first kanji spelling, or kana if it's "usually kana" or the lemma is kana. Entries with no JMdict match are dropped (299 fragments like 甲状/思春/各号).
- Notes in the header comment state the exclusions.

**`tools/build_freq_extra.py <addon_dir> <suw.tsv|zip> <luw.zip>`** → `freq_extra.txt` (2026-10-08)
- Long-unit list: NINJAL **record 3228**, `BCCWJ_frequencylist_luw_ver1_0.zip` (57 MB zip / 521 MB TSV; read straight from the zip). Short-unit zip is `data/bccwj_suw.zip`.
- `x` rows: exact SUW ranks 20,001–40,000. `e` rows: LUW-only words (JMdict entries the SUW list never ranks, e.g. 日本語 ≈#1,662, 可能性 ≈#506, 具体的 ≈#799) with an **estimated rank** = where their per-million frequency (same denominator, laws excluded) falls in the SUW ranking. Nothing is inserted, so freq_bccwj.txt, tiers and coverage are unchanged (the script asserts the top 20,000 match). Capped at rank 40,000 (36k rows, 1.6 MB).
- Runtime: `freq_data.word_rank_info()` / `rank_text()` ("≈#1,662"), `coverage()["nid_rank_extra"]`. Used only for display and sorting (Off-list tiles/table, JLPT grid sort, Missing Words). Kana cards only match an extra entry spelled in that kana.
- Still unranked by design: set phrases LUW also splits (本当に, 今まで, について, ありがとうございます), dates/numbers (二月), proper nouns (東京). Off-list on the owner's deck (2026-10-08, after all additions): 781 = 364 top-20k, 79 exact beyond, 123 estimated, 215 unranked.
- Known gap: UniDic lemmas conflate some kanji (抑える counts under 押さえる), so a 抑える card stays unranked.

**`tools/build_kanji.py <addon_dir>`** → `kanji_jlpt.txt` (2026-10-09)
- Source: OpenJLPT `data/json/kanji/n{1..5}.json` (downloaded to `%TEMP%\ojlpt\kanji\`; grammar JSON is in `%TEMP%\ojlpt\grammar\`). 2,383 kanji, no duplicates across levels: N5 84, N4 169, N3 387, N2 398, N1 1,345. Ordered by level, then KANJIDIC2 newspaper frequency (none → last).
- **`supp`** = OpenJLPT's "supplementary" kanji (172): not on Waller's kanji list but needed by that level's vocabulary (分/無/誰 at N5, 的/可/身 at N3). Kept, like our word additions.

**`tools/build_grammar.py <addon_dir>`** → `grammar_jlpt.json` (2026-10-09): OpenJLPT `data/json/grammar/n{1..5}.json` (in `%TEMP%\ojlpt\grammar\`), 526 points, first example only. No per-point page exists on the OpenJLPT site (404), so Shift+click on a grammar tile is a Google search ("pattern 文法 JLPT Nx").

**Investigated and deliberately NOT done**
- Excluding white papers / Diet minutes / PR newsletters: they hold N2/N1 reading vocabulary (整備, 推進, 措置) and notices vocabulary (午前, 募集).
- Compound-fragment filtering via the long-unit list (会, 県, 者, 率 rank high because BCCWJ splits compounds): the owner said not to bother.
- Counters (年, 月, 円, 回) stay; they're legitimate JLPT vocabulary.

## 7. Decision log (what was chosen, and why; alternatives rejected)

| Topic | Decision | Rejected alternatives / reasoning |
|---|---|---|
| JLPT source | **OpenJLPT** + JMdict enrichment + `ADDITIONS` (Waller words OpenJLPT dropped) + `COMMUNITY_ADDITIONS` (211, flagged `community`) | Old japandict scrape (22 archaic forms at N5); **stephenmk/yomitan-jlpt-vocab** (still 12 archaic at N5, ありがとう only at N3). All are Waller-based. |
| Kana vs kanji cards | Kanji card → kana entry OK; kana card → kanji entry never; usually-kana words listed in kana | The strict rule alone dropped うるさい, むしろ, さすが and similar (normally written in kana), so the kana relisting was added. |
| Stricter kana relisting (only relist when the kanji are non-jōyō, e.g. 煩/寧/鼠, keeping 分かる/全て/眼鏡 kanji-only) | **Not built; owner chose to keep the current behaviour** | Prompted by a claim (via Gemini) that the JLPT shows some usually-kana words in kanji at higher levels. Partly true, but no official kanji guide exists since 2010, and kanji cards always count anyway, so the only over-credit is kana-only cards for ~275 words. The stricter version would need KANJIDIC2, and it's imperfect (流石 uses jōyō kanji but is written さすが). |
| Homophones (うん ↛ 運, せい ↛ 性/正) | Kana cards never count for different words that sound the same | Confirmed correct by the owner. |
| List-source setting ("old official vs modern") | **Not built** | Both options are the same Waller data; a toggle would also orphan overrides keyed by spelling. |
| Frequency source | **BCCWJ** (written, balanced, closest to JLPT reading); long-unit list only for ≈ display ranks | Jiten (CC BY-SA, modern but ~90% anime/drama/novels/games); JPDB (no license). A BCCWJ+Jiten blend is backlog #12 (on hold). Merging LUW into the SUW ranking was rejected: it would shift every rank and double-count (日本語 is already counted in 日本 + 語). |
| BCCWJ registers | Exclude **laws only** | See §6. |
| BCCWJ reading/kanji splits (2026-10-09) | `freq_data._merge_reading_splits` at **load time** (bundled file untouched): rows with the same display + same JMdict spelling set are one word → per-million summed, best row's reading kept, re-sorted, topped back up to 20,000 from freq_extra `x` rows. 74 merges (明日 あす#700/あした#8,117/みょうにち → #641; 今日 → #122; 町/街 まち → #260). Homographs with different spellings (居る/要る, 成る/鳴る) stay separate. `LIST_VERSION` feeds `history.signature()`. Merged-away readings are kept in `MERGED_READINGS` (`merged_readings()`): frequency-grid hover shows 明日 あす・あした・みょうにち and grid search matches them (owner: searching あした found nothing). Frequency-grid hover shows a JLPT level only on the row the JLPT entry's `word_rank` points to (`_JLPT_LEVEL_OF_RANK`), plus a note when level and frequency range differ. | Owner saw N5 明日 in the N1 frequency range. Rejected: forcing JLPT words into their level's range (breaks "Up to N3 = top 3,900", and the JLPT bars already show the official view). Blend with Jiten stays backlog #12: a single blended file would be a derivative of NC-ND + BY-SA, which can't be combined; a runtime blend of separately shipped files is the idea to check before public release. |
| One tile per word (2026-10-12, refines the row above) | **Different meaning or use → own tile; same word with another reading or a same-meaning kanji spelling → one tile.** All decisions in `freq_splits.py`, each with a one-line meaning note: KIND1_SPLITS (24 corpus-separate words JMdict merged: 町 town / 街 street, 足 foot / 脚 leg …), READING_SPLITS (今日 today / nowadays, 汚す dirty / defile, 避ける avoid / dodge, 汁 soup / broth), SHARED_SPELLINGS (86 spellings the corpus lumps: 聞く / 聴く listen attentively, 返る / 帰る go home, 取る / 撮る photograph …, placed right after their parent at its frequency, with their own meanings). Kept merged on purpose: 明日 あした/あす and the other reading splits, 年配/年輩, 唯/只, 柔らかい/軟らかい, 分かる/解る/判る, 言う/云う, 承ける (stays with 受ける) … | **Learner's reason (owner: "those have different meanings and should have their own cards"):** a tile is something you learn once. Recognising 聞く doesn't mean you understand 聴く, and knowing 返る doesn't cover 帰る, so one tile for both would show a word as known when half of it isn't, and each deserves its own card. Knowing either spelling of 年配/年輩 is the same knowledge, so splitting those would demand redundant cards. Rejected: Fix 1b, counting a 帰る card toward 返る (the corpus's lumping), for that reason. Still offered, not chosen: 良い/善い/好い, 因る/依る/拠る/由る, 内/中, 付く/附く. See also the owner's learning philosophy (recognition-first Anki). |
| Kana homophones in frequency | One kana card = one word | It previously counted そう as 10 words. |
| Loanwords | Button marks *found*, excluding wasei/false friends/abbr/non-English (dated words now included). **Since 2026-10-08 it also covers the frequency list** (`_loanword_pairs`: JLPT pairs + frequency entries with no card and `freq_data.is_english_loanword`; 386 JLPT + 1,778 frequency-only on the owner's deck). **Owner review (2026-10-08):** JMdict's abbr tag (320 words on our lists) was too broad, so tags no longer decide alone. `COUNT_AS_ENGLISH` (291 words, any tag: テレビ, アニメ, エアコン, リモコン, パトカー, ボールペン …) is always marked; `FALSE_FRIENDS` (23: テンション, センス, ストーブ, ワンピース, パンツ, スタイル, ニート, ラムネ …) never. The owner moved クレーム, マスター, プロレス, サービス, ファミコン etc. OUT of false friends (guessable). Explicitly not English: ハンカチ, パート, ファイト, ハーフ, ハンバーグ, レンジ, モーニング, ソープ, リュック, メタボ, アルバイト, パソコン. ラジカセ/ワープロ are also in DATED_WORDS, which wins (asked the owner). | The owner's earlier "suspend all katakana" was too blunt. |
| Dated words | ~~Hand-picked 16-word list (`DATED_WORDS`) + Suspend Dated Words button~~ **Removed 2026-10-09**; the 15 katakana ones moved to `COUNT_AS_ENGLISH` (marked found by the loanword button), 電報 is now an ordinary word | Owner: a button for 15 words isn't worth it. |
| False friends | Hand-picked 15, **+21 on 2026-10-08** from a review of Wikipedia's "List of gairaigo and wasei-eigo terms" (579 words; only the 108 the loanword button marked matter): meaning in Japanese ≠ English source (クレーム, テンション, センス, リフォーム, リベンジ, モーテル, ペンション, ストーブ, トレーナー, ワンピース, シール, ヒップ, パンク, グラビア, エール, リーチ, マニア) or unguessable shortenings (プロレス, ファミコン, サンド, エキス). 16 borderline words sent to the owner; **owner marked 6 as wasei (added)**: サービス, スタイル, パンツ, クラクション, ニート, ラムネ; kept as normal loanwords: サイン, ジュース, ドライバー, フロント, デスク, チャージ, アンチ, バッティング, リニューアル, スピン. Total now 42. Original 15 (`FALSE_FRIENDS`: ワイシャツ, マンション, スマート, ハンドル, カンニング, トランプ, ミシン, ジャンパー, タレント, パチンコ, ルーズ, ボーイ, マスター, プリント, ヤング) | JMdict only tags some wasei-eigo. |
| Abbreviations (パソコン, テレビ…) | Excluded from auto-mark | Not reliably guessable. |
| Frequency target label | "Top 13,400" (`N1_VOCAB_TARGET = 13_400`) | 13,402 was a rounding artifact of the rank table. |
| Amaterasu art in the rank legend | **Blurred with a faint "?"** (`_mystery_pixmap`: shrink ×18/×9/×4 + smooth upscale; "?" 92% of the art height, 37% white fill, thin dark 22% outline + 7% halo so it stays readable but subtle on gold and on the black hair) until the user's (previewed) count reaches Amaterasu; tooltip "Reach this rank to reveal it" | Owner wants Amaterasu to be a surprise. The name stays visible in the legend. |
| Frequency tiers | **Modern JLPT vocab estimates** (N5 800, N4 1,500, N3 3,900, N2 6,000, N1 13,400) + Near-native 20,000; grid sections and window titles named the same | Owner: the old 1k/5k/10k cut-offs were arbitrary goal posts. Keep in sync with the rank legend's milestone list. |
| 20,000 = "Near Native" (checked 2026-10-08) | Main Frequency Check bar labelled **Near Native / 20,000**; tooltip "Near-native active vocabulary (estimate)". Research: native *active* (productive) vocabulary estimates range ~8,000 (Aramaki et al. 2012, IPSJ, online writing) to ~30,000 (Ogiwara 2016, Bukkyo Univ., university seniors); *recognition* ~40,000–45,000 (Ogiwara; VSTRJ-50K studies). 20,000 sits inside the active range and well below recognition, so it's defensible as "near-native active", not total, vocabulary. **Owner's definition of active:** a word you've produced (written or spoken) more than a handful of times in your life. Under that lifetime definition the ~8,000 online-writing figure is a floor (one medium, one sample); the ~30,000 self-report figure is the closer match, so 20,000 reads as *near* (below a typical educated native), not full, native. **Decision (2026-10-08): keep 20,000 "Near Native".** Rejected: a 30,000 "Native" target (best estimate for full native active vocab is ~25–30k, but unmeasured; BCCWJ entries past 20k are noisy; frequency ≠ native vocabulary shape; would break the link with the rank's 20,000 max). | Owner asked to verify; it's an estimate, not a measured standard. |
| Frequency target wording | **Superseded 2026-10-20 (owner): the frequency bars are named by size ("Top 800" … "Top 13,400"); their sizes follow the modern estimates per JLPT level, but the words are written Japanese's, not the test's.** Was: always "Required N1 Vocab (Modern Estimate)"; never mention Amaterasu in the frequency context (description, tooltips, README) | Owner wants Amaterasu to be a surprise, discovered by reaching it or in the rank legend (the "?" popup may name it). Constant renamed from `AMATERASU_START`. |
| Word Grid layout | JLPT words only: known (level → maturity → frequency) then missing; tab named "Word Grid". The old Non-JLPT section was removed 2026-10-08 (lives in the Off-list tab). | Owner's explicit spec. |

Missing tiles keep their old order. Each grid's subtext states the order (`GROUPING_TEXT`). The Non-JLPT tab's table stays plain frequency order. | Owner request.
